{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}q^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{E^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{q^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}f}

Proof.

For starters, we prove that
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
satisfies
[VC.1]
. Let us compute the following equalities:

\displaystyle\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{R}^{\prime}\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}g

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}g\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}

(
\text{Definition of }\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{V}_{f}g\iota_{q}\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}

(
\mathrm{V}_{f}g\iota_{q}=\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}g
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{V}_{f}g\iota_{q}

(
[VC.1]
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}g

(
\mathrm{V}_{f}g\iota_{q}=\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}g
)

Furthermore:

\displaystyle\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{R}^{\prime}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}q^{\prime}p_{M}

(
\text{Lemma}\penalty\ \ref{lemma:vertical-connections}.(a)
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q^{\prime}z_{M}p_{M}

(
\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}q^{\prime}=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q^{\prime}z_{M}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q^{\prime}

(
z_{M}p_{M}=\mathsf{id}_{M}
)

Thus, by the universal property of the two pullback diagrams, we conclude that
\iota_{q}^{\prime}\mathrm{R}^{\prime}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{V}q^{\prime}}
. Next, let us prove
[VC.2]
. First, let us prove that
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}q^{\prime\mathrm{V}}=p_{E^{\prime}}
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{\prime}q^{\prime\mathrm{V}}g

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{R}^{\prime}\iota_{q^{\prime}}p_{E^{\prime}}g

(
q^{\prime\mathrm{V}}=\iota_{q^{\prime}}p_{E^{\prime}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{R}^{\prime}\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}gp_{E}

(
\text{Naturality of }p
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}g\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}p_{E}

(
\text{Definition of }\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}gp_{E}

(
[VC.1]
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ p_{E^{\prime}}g

(
\text{Naturality of }p
)

Moreover, we can also write

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{\prime}q^{\prime\mathrm{V}}q^{\prime}=\mathrm{R}^{\prime}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q^{\prime}=p_{E^{\prime}}q^{\prime}

where we used that
q^{\prime\mathrm{V}}q^{\prime}=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q^{\prime}
and again
[VC.1]
. Thus, by the universal property of the pullback,
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}q^{\prime\mathrm{V}}=p_{E^{\prime}}
. Finally, let us prove linearity:

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{\prime}l_{q^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{V}}\mathrm{T}\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}^{2}g

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{R}^{\prime}\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}gl_{E}

(
\text{Naturality of $l$ and linearity of }\iota_{q^{\prime}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}g\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}l_{E}

(
\text{Definition of }\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}gl_{E}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{R}\mathrm{T}\iota_{q}

(
[VC.2]
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ l_{E}\mathrm{T}^{2}g\mathrm{T}\mathrm{R}\mathrm{T}\iota_{q}

(
\text{Naturality of $l$ and linearity of }\iota_{q^{\prime}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ l_{E}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{R}^{\prime}\mathrm{T}\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}^{2}g

(
\text{Definition of }\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
)

Moreover, we also compute

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{\prime}l_{q^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{V}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q^{\prime}=\mathrm{R}^{\prime}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q^{\prime}z_{M^{\prime}}=p_{E}q^{\prime}z_{M}=\mathrm{T}q^{\prime}p_{M}z_{M}=\mathrm{T}q^{\prime}l_{M}p_{\mathrm{T}M}=l_{E^{\prime}}p_{E^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}q^{\prime}

where we used the naturality of
l
, of
p
, that
lp_{\mathrm{T}}=pz
, and
[VC.2]
. Thus, by the universal property of the two tangent pullbacks, we conclude that
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}l_{q^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{V}}=l_{E^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
, thus, that
[VC.2]
holds for
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
.
∎

Thanks to stability under pullbacks, vertical connections can be organized into a tangent fibration.

Proposition 3.6
.

The codomain functor
\Pi\colon\mathsf{VC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which sends a vertical connection
(q,\mathrm{R})
to the codomain
M
of
q\colon E\to M
is a tangent fibration.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the stability under the tangent bundle functor and along tangent pullbacks of tangent display maps and of vertical connections (see
[
7
, Proposition 3.11]
).
∎

In general, tangent display maps are not stable under retration, however, they become stable when the idempotents of the tangent category split
[
7
, Corollary 2.25]
. Concretely, this means that, if the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{M}}

(3.1)

commutes and
(s_{E},r_{E})
and
(s_{M},r_{M})
are section-retraction pairs, that is,
s_{E}r_{E}=\mathsf{id}_{E^{\prime}}
and
s_{M}r_{M}=\mathsf{id}_{M^{\prime}}
, then, if
q
is a tangent display map, so is
q^{\prime}
. The next result shows that, under the condition of tangent display maps to be stable under retration, also vertical connections are too.

Proposition 3.7
.

In a tangent category whose tangent display maps are closed under retraction, vertical connections are also closed under retraction. Explicitly, if
\mathrm{R}
is a vertical connection on a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
and
(s_{E},r_{E})
and
(s_{M},r_{M})
are section-retraction pairs as in Equation (
3.1
), then the tangent display map
q^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=s_{E}qr_{M}\colon E^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}
comes with a vertical connection
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{T}E^{\prime}\to\mathrm{V}q^{\prime}
defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{T}E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}s_{E}}\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{V}q\xrightarrow{\mathrm{V}_{r_{M}}r_{E}}\mathrm{V}q^{\prime}

Proof.

To show that
[VC.1]
holds for
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
, let us compute the following:

\displaystyle\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{R}^{\prime}=\iota_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}s_{E}\mathrm{R}\mathrm{V}_{r_{M}}r_{E}=\mathrm{V}_{s_{M}}s_{E}\iota_{q}\mathrm{R}\mathrm{V}_{r_{M}}r_{E}=\mathrm{V}_{s_{M}}s_{E}\mathrm{V}_{r_{M}}r_{E}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{V}q^{\prime}}

To prove
[VC.2]
, let us start by computing the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{\prime}q^{\prime\mathrm{V}}=\mathrm{T}s_{E}\mathrm{R}\mathrm{V}_{r_{M}}r_{E}\iota_{q^{\prime}}p_{E^{\prime}}=\mathrm{T}s_{E}\mathrm{R}\mathrm{T}r_{E}p_{E^{\prime}}=\mathrm{T}s_{E}\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}p_{E}r_{E}=\mathrm{T}s_{E}p_{E}r_{E}=p_{E^{\prime}}s_{E}r_{E}=p_{E^{\prime}}

where we used that
\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}p_{E}=\mathrm{R}q^{\mathrm{V}}=p_{E}
. This shows that
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
is a bundle morphism. To prove that
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
is linear, notice that,
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
is composition of linear morphisms, since
\mathrm{T}s_{E}
,
\mathrm{R}
, and
\mathrm{V}_{r_{M}}r_{E}
are all linear. Thus,
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
is also linear and
[VC.2]
holds.
∎

3.2
Horizontal connections

Generally, a vertical connection is insufficient for specifying the horizontal bundle of a connection. In fact, in the absence of negatives as in the general case of a tangent category, the kernel of
\mathrm{R}
may fail to be in direct sum with the vertical bundle. For this reason, an Ehresmann connection comprises a second piece of information: a horizontal connection. In this section, we introduce this concept in tangent categories and study some of its properties.

Definition 3.8
.

A
horizontal connection
on a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
consists of a morphism
\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
satisfying the following conditions:

​[HC.1]:

The map
\mathrm{H}
is a section of the horizontal descent
\pi_{q}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{q}}

​[HC.2]:

The morphism
\mathrm{H}\colon q^{\mathrm{F}}\to\mathbf{p}_{E}
is a linear morphism of differential bundles over
E
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{E}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{E}}

[HC.1]
requires that
\mathrm{H}
is an inclusion of the Finsler bundle
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to E
into the tangent bundle of
E
;
[HC.2]
requires that this inclusion is linear.

We denote by
\mathsf{HC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
the tangent category of pairs
(q,\mathrm{H})
formed by a tangent display map
q
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and a horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}
on
q
, and commutative squares
(f,g)\colon q\to q^{\prime}
for morphisms, that is,
qf=gq^{\prime}
, compatible with the horizontal connections as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{F}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{\prime}}

The tangent bundle functor of
\mathsf{HC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
sends each
(q,\mathrm{H})
to
(\mathrm{T}q,\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}})
, where

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}}\colon\mathrm{F}\mathrm{T}q\xrightarrow{\gamma^{\mathrm{F}}_{q}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{F}q\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{H}}\mathrm{T}^{2}E\xrightarrow{c}\mathrm{T}^{2}E

and
\gamma^{\mathrm{F}}_{q}\colon\mathrm{T}\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{F}\mathrm{T}q
is the strong distributive law of the tangent morphism
(-)^{\mathrm{F}}
of Lemma
2.26
.

Lemma 3.9
.

If
\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
is a horizontal connection on a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
, then the morphism
\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q\to\mathrm{T}q
is a bundle morphism:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}

Proof.

Recall that
\pi_{q}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q=\mathrm{T}q
, thus, we compute:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}\mathrm{T}q

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}\pi_{q}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q

(
\mathrm{T}q=\pi_{q}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q

(
[HC.1]
)

This concludes the proof.
∎

Thanks to
[HC.1]
, a horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
is naturally associated with a linear idempotent
\psi_{\mathrm{H}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}E
on the tangent bundle, called the horizontal connection form of
\mathrm{H}
. Just as with the vertical connection form, we formally introduce this concept.

Definition 3.10
.

A
horizontal connection form
of a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
consists of an endomorphism
\psi\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}E
, subject to the following conditions:

​[HCF.1]:

The following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{q}}

​[HCF.2]:

The morphism
\psi\colon\mathbf{p}_{E}\to\mathbf{p}_{E}
is a linear endomorphism of differential bundles;

​[HCF.3]:

The following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\mathrm{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{E}}

The next theorem proves that if the bundle map is a submersion, the information of a horizontal connection is entirely retained in its horizontal connection form. In particular, to reconstruct a horizontal connection from a horizontal connection form, we make use of the universal property of the pushout diagram of Definition
2.29
.

Theorem 3.11
.

There is a bijective correspondence between horizontal connections and horizontal connection forms on a submersion
q\colon E\to M
. Concretely, if
\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
is a horizontal connection on a submersion
q\colon E\to M
, the morphism

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathrm{H}}\colon\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{\pi_{q}}\mathrm{F}q\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}E}\mathrm{T}E

is a horizontal connection form of
q
. Conversely, if
\psi\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}E
is a horizontal connection form on a submersion
q\colon E\to M
, the unique morphism
\mathrm{H}_{\psi}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
of
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
rendering the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\mathrm{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{\psi}}

commutative is a horizontal connection on
q
.

Proof.

For starters, assume that
\mathrm{H}
is a horizontal connection and let us prove that
\psi_{\mathrm{H}}
is in fact a horizontal connection form. For this, by
[HC.1]
,
\psi_{\mathrm{H}}\pi_{q}=\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}\pi_{q}=\pi_{q}
, thus,
[HCF.1]
holds. To prove
[HCF.2]
, notice that
\psi_{\mathrm{H}}
is composition of two linear morphisms,
\mathrm{H}
and
\pi_{q}
, thus, it is also linear. Finally, we compute the following:

\displaystyle\iota_{q}\psi_{\mathrm{H}}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \iota_{q}\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}

(
\text{Definition of }\psi_{\mathrm{H}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{V}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{H}

(
\iota_{q}\pi_{q}=q^{\mathrm{V}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}},\text{Lemma}\penalty\ \ref{lemma:vertical-bundle-kernel}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{V}}z_{E}

(
\mathrm{H}\text{ is linear, thus it preserves the zero}
)

Therefore,
[HCF.3]
holds. Conversely, assume that
\psi
is a horizontal connection form and let us prove that
\mathrm{H}_{\psi}
is a horizontal connection. For starters, notice that by
[HCF.3]
and thanks to the linearity of
\psi
guaranteed by
[HCF.2]
,
\mathrm{H}_{\psi}
is well-defined. Moreover,
\mathrm{H}_{\psi}
satisfies
[HC.2]
since the morphisms of
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
are linear. To prove
[HC.1]
, notice that
\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}_{\psi}\pi_{q}=\psi\pi_{q}=\pi_{q}
, where we used
[HCF.1]
. Moreover,
z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{H}_{\psi}\pi_{q}=z_{E}\pi_{q}=z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}
, thus, by the universal property of the pushout diagram,
\mathrm{H}_{\psi}\pi_{q}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{F}q}
, that is,
[HC.1]
holds. Finally, let us prove that these two operations are inverse to each other. Start with a horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}
. Thus,
\mathrm{H}_{\psi_{\mathrm{H}}}
is the unique morphism such that
\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}_{\psi_{\mathrm{H}}}=\psi_{\mathrm{H}}=\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}
and since
\pi_{q}
is epi,
\mathrm{H}
must coincides with
\mathrm{H}_{\psi_{\mathrm{H}}}
. Now, let us consider a horizontal connection form
\psi
. Thus,
\psi_{\mathrm{H}_{\psi}}=\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}_{\psi}
, which, by definition, is just
\psi
.
∎

Similar to vertical connections, horizontal connections are also stable under pullbacks. To prove this result we first need a technical lemma.

Lemma 3.12
.

Consider a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
and the tangent pullback diagram of
q
along a morphism
f\colon M^{\prime}\to M
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Thus, the following diagram commutes, and it is also a tangent pullback diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{F}_{f}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}f}

Proof.

The diagram commutes since, by definition of
\mathrm{F}_{f}g
,
\mathrm{F}_{f}g\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q\mathrm{T}f
. To prove that the diagram is also a tangent pullback, consider the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{F}_{f}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{M}}

The right square is a tangent pullback by definition of the Finsler bundle. Notice that the outer diagram can be rewritten as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{M^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

However, this diagram is two tangent pullback diagrams stacked together. Thus, the outer rectangle of the previous diagram is also a tangent pullback, and by Lemma
2.6
, so is the left one.
∎

Proposition 3.13
.

If
q\colon E\to M
is a tangent display map,
\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
a horizontal connection on
q
, and
f\colon M^{\prime}\to M
is a morphism, the tangent display map
q^{\prime}\colon E^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}
, pullback of
q
along
f

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

comes with a horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{F}q^{\prime}\to\mathrm{T}E^{\prime}
, defined as the unique morphism rendering the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{F}_{f}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}f}

commutative.

Proof.

To begin, let us prove that
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}
satisfies
[HC.1]
. Let us compute the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\pi_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{F}_{f}g

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}^{\prime}\mathrm{T}g\pi_{q}

(
\pi_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{F}_{f}g=\mathrm{T}g\pi_{q}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{F}_{f}g\mathrm{H}\pi_{q}

(
\text{Definition of }\mathrm{H}^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{F}_{f}g

(
[HC.1]
)

Moreover, we can also compute:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\pi_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q=\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\mathrm{T}q^{\prime}=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q

Thus, by the universal property of the tangent pullback diagram of Lemma
3.12
,
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\pi_{q^{\prime}}
must be
\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{F}q^{\prime}}
, that is,
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}
satisfies
[HC.1]
. To prove
[HC.2]
, let us start by showing that
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}p_{E^{\prime}}=\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\pi_{q^{\prime}}q^{\prime\mathrm{F}}=q^{\prime\mathrm{F}}
, where we used that
p_{E^{\prime}}=\pi_{q^{\prime}}q^{\prime\mathrm{F}}
. Finally, let us prove linearity by using again the universal property of the tangent pullback diagram of Lemma
3.12
. We start by computing the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\prime}l_{E^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}^{2}g

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}^{\prime}\mathrm{T}gl_{E}

(
\text{Naturality of }l
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{F}_{f}g\mathrm{H}l_{E}

(
\text{Definition of }\mathrm{H}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{F}_{f}gl_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{H}

(
[HC.2]
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ l_{q^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{F}_{f}g\mathrm{T}\mathrm{H}

(
\text{Linearity of }\mathrm{F}_{f}g
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ l_{q^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\mathrm{T}^{2}g

(
\text{Definition of }\mathrm{H}
)

Moreover, we compute:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\prime}l_{E^{\prime}}\mathrm{T}^{2}q^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}^{\prime}\mathrm{T}q^{\prime}l_{M^{\prime}}

(
\text{Naturality of }l
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}ql_{M^{\prime}}

(
\text{Definition of }\mathrm{H}^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ l_{q^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q

(
\text{Linearity of }\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ l_{q^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\mathrm{T}^{2}q^{\prime}

(
\text{Definition of }\mathrm{H}^{\prime}
)

Thus, we conclude that
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}l_{E^{\prime}}=l_{q^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{H}^{\prime}
, that is,
[HC.2]
holds.
∎

Thanks to stability under pullbacks, horizontal connections form a tangent fibration.

Proposition 3.14
.

The codomain functor
\Pi\colon\mathsf{HC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which sends a horizontal connection
(q,\mathrm{H})
to the codomain
M
of
q\colon E\to M
is a tangent fibration.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the stability under the tangent bundle functor and along tangent pullbacks of tangent display maps and of horizontal connections (see
[
7
, Proposition 3.11]
).
∎

As with vertical connections, horizontal connections are stable under retraction.

Proposition 3.15
.

Whenenever tangent display maps are closed under retraction, horizontal connections are also closed under retraction. Explicitly, if
\mathrm{H}
is a horizontal connection on a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
and
(s_{E},r_{E})
and
(s_{M},r_{M})
are section-retraction pairs as in Equation (
3.1
), then the tangent display map
q^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=s_{E}qr_{M}\colon E^{\prime}\to M
comes with a horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{F}q^{\prime}\to\mathrm{T}E^{\prime}
defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{F}q^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{F}_{s_{M}}s_{E}}\mathrm{F}q\xrightarrow{\mathrm{H}}\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}r_{E}}\mathrm{T}E^{\prime}

Proof.

The proof is fairly similar to that of Proposition
3.7
; therefore, we leave it to the reader to complete the details.
∎

3.3
Ehresmann connections

A vertical connection together with a horizontal connection, subject to two compatibility conditions specify an Ehresmann connection. In this section, we introduce Ehresmann connections in tangent categories.

Definition 3.16
.

An
Ehresmann connection
of a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
is a pair
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
consisting of a vertical connection
\mathrm{R}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{V}q
of
q
and a horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{F}q
of
q
subject to the following conditions:

​[EC.1]:

The following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}}

​[EC.2]:

The following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{E}}

Axiom
[EC.1]
requires that the image of the horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}
is in the kernel of the vertical connection
\mathrm{R}
;
[EC.2]
requires that every tangent vector of
E
is a sum of a vertical vector and a horizontal vector. Together,
[EC.1]
and
[EC.2]
establish that the horizontal and the vertical connections are in direct sum with each other.

We denote by
\mathsf{EC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
the tangent category of triples
(q;\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
formed by a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
and by an Ehresmann connection
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
of
q
. A morphism of
\mathsf{EC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
consists of a bundle morphism
(f,g)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
which is at the same time a morphism of vertical connections and a morphism of horizontal connections. The tangent bundle functor sends a triple
(q;\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
to
(\mathrm{T}q;\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{T}},\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}})
, where
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{T}}
and
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{T}}
are defined as in
\mathsf{VC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{HC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, respectively.

Vertical and horizontal connections are in bijective correspondence with vertical and horizontal connection forms. Similarly, an Ehresmann connection is equivalent to an Ehresmann connection form. In the next definition, we make this concept precise.

Definition 3.17
.

An
Ehresmann connection form
on a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
is a pair
(\phi,\psi)
consisting of a vertical connection form
\phi\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}E
and a horizontal connection form
\psi\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}E
on
q
, subject to the following conditions:

​[ECF.1]:

The following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{E}}

​[ECF.2]:

The following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\phi,\psi\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{E}}

The correspondence between vertical connections and vertical connection forms and and one between horizontal connections and horizontal connection forms extend to a correspondence between Ehresmann connections and Ehresmann connection forms.

Theorem 3.18
.

There is a bijective correspondence between Ehresmann connections and Ehresmann connection forms of a submersion
q\colon E\to M
. Explicitly, if
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
is a connection on
q\colon E\to M
, the morphisms
\phi_{\mathrm{R}}
and
\psi_{\mathrm{H}}
of Theorems
3.4
and
3.11
form a connection form
(\phi_{\mathrm{R}},\psi_{\mathrm{H}})
of
q
. Conversely, if
(\phi,\psi)
is an Ehresmann connection form on
q\colon E\to M
, the morphisms
\mathrm{R}_{\phi}
and
\mathrm{H}_{\psi}
of Theorems
3.4
and
3.11
define an Ehresmann connection
(\mathrm{R}_{\phi},\mathrm{H}_{\psi})
of
q
.

Proof.

It is not hard to see that, in the correspondences of Theorems
3.4
and
3.11
, axioms
[EC.1]
and
[EC.2]
correspond precisely to
[ECF.1]
and
[ECF.2]
, respectively.
∎

From the stability under pullbacks of vertical and horizontal connections, it follows that Ehresmann connections are also stable under pullbacks.

Proposition 3.19
.

Ehresmann connections are closed under pullbacks. Concretely, given a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
and a morphism
f\colon M^{\prime}\to M
, the tangent display map
q^{\prime}\colon E^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}
pullback of
q
along
f
carries an Ehresmann connection
(\mathrm{R}^{\prime},\mathrm{H}^{\prime})
provided that
q
does, where
\mathrm{R}^{\prime}
and
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}
are the vertical and horizontal connections of Propositions
3.5
and
3.12
, respectively.

Proof.

We leave it to the reader to complete this proof.
∎

Thanks to stability under pullbacks, Ehresmann connections form a tangent fibration.

Proposition 3.20
.

The codomain functor
\Pi\colon\mathsf{EC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which sends a Ehresmann connection
(q,\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
to the codomain
M
of
q\colon E\to M
is a tangent fibration.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the stability under the tangent bundle functor and along tangent pullbacks of tangent display maps and of Ehresmann connections.
∎

3.4
Ehresmann connections and the fundamental short exact sequence

We may now connect our definition of an Ehresmann connection with one of the standard definitions in differential geometry. We will prove that an Ehresmann connection is precisely a splitting of the fundamental short exact sequence of a submersion, which implies that an Ehresmann connection is a splitting of the tangent bundle into a direct sum of the vertical bundle and a horizontal bundle. In a semi-additive category, a
splitting
of a short (not necessarily exact) sequence

\displaystyle 0\to A\xrightarrow{\iota}B\xrightarrow{\pi}C\to 0

consists of two morphisms
\sigma\colon C\to B
and
\rho\colon B\to A
such that,
\sigma
is a section of
\pi\colon B\to C
, that is,
\sigma\pi=\mathsf{id}_{C}
and
\rho
is a retraction of
\iota\colon A\to B
, that is,
\iota\rho=\mathsf{id}_{A}
. Furthermore,
\sigma
and
\rho
must be in direct sum with each other, that is, the following diagrams must commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B\oplus B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\rho\iota,\pi\sigma\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces+}

where
+
denotes the unique morphism
[\mathsf{id}_{B},\mathsf{id}_{B}]
induced by the universal property of the biproduct
B\oplus B
.

Theorem 3.21
.

Given a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
, the following statements are equivalent:

[1]

(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
is an Ehresmann connection on
q
;

[2]

(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
is a splitting of the fundamental short exact sequence of
q
.

Proof.

Consider an Ehresmann connection
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
on a submersion
q
. By
[VC.1]
and
[HC.1]
,
\mathrm{R}
is a retraction of the inclusion
\iota_{q}\colon\mathrm{V}q\to\mathrm{T}E
and
\mathrm{H}
is a section of the horizontal descent
\pi_{q}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{F}q
. Moreover, by
[VC.2]
and
[HC.2]
,
\mathrm{R}
and
\mathrm{H}
are morphisms of
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
. Finally, by
[EC.1]
and
[EC.2]
,
\mathrm{R}
and
\mathrm{H}
are in direct sum with each other. Conversely, consider a splitting
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
of the fundamental short sequence of
q
. Since
\mathrm{R}
is a retraction of
\iota_{q}
,
\mathrm{R}
satisfies
[VC.1]
. Moreover,
\mathrm{R}
is linear, thus,
\mathrm{R}
satisfies also
[VC.2]
. Similarly, since
\mathrm{H}
is a linear section of
\pi_{q}
,
\mathrm{H}
satisfies both
[HC.1]
and
[HC.2]
. Finally, by the orthogonality of
\mathrm{R}
and
\mathrm{H}
, the pair
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
satisfies
[EC.1]
and
[EC.2]
. Thus,
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
is an Ehresmann connection on
q
.
∎

Thanks to Theorem
3.21
, we can now make precise an important point. Altough the fundamental short exact sequence of Definition
2.32
is a well-defined sequence for every tangent display map, only for submersions is it exact. However, if a tangent display map
q
has an Ehresmann connection,
q
becomes a submersion, automatically.

Corollary 3.22
.

If a tangent display map
q
admits an Ehresmann connection, then
q
is a submersion.

Proof.

This result is a consequence of a general phenomenon of short sequences. Suppose in fact that
0\to A\to B\to C\to 0
is a sequence in a semi-additive category. If this sequence admits a splitting, the sequence is necessarily exact. To prove this, let us call the left morphism of the sequence
\iota\colon A\to B
and the right morphism,
\pi\colon B\to C
. If the sequence admits a splitting
\rho
and
\sigma
, then,
\iota
and
\pi
become respectively a slit mono and a split epi morphism. Therefore, the sequence becomes exact in
A
and
C
. To prove that it is also exact in
B
, one needs to show that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

is both a pullback and a pushout. We prove that the diagram is a pushout since by Lemma
2.27
, we already know that the one of the fundamental short sequence of a tangent display map is always a pullback. However, for the general case, the proof can be done dually. Consider two morphisms
\alpha\colon 0\to X
and
\beta\colon B\to X
subject to the condition that
\iota\beta=0\alpha
, where
0\colon A\to 0
. However, since
0
is a zero object, there is a unique map
0_{A,X}\colon A\to X
that factors through
0
, the zero map. By the orthogonality condition of the splitting,
\rho\iota+\pi\sigma=\mathsf{id}_{B}
. Therefore, we compute:

\displaystyle\beta=(\rho\iota+\pi\sigma)\beta=\rho\iota\beta+\pi\sigma\beta=0_{B,X}+\pi\sigma\beta=\pi\sigma\beta

where we used the additivity of
\beta
. Therefore,
\sigma\beta\colon C\to X
satisfies the following equations.
\pi\sigma\beta=\beta
,
0_{0,C}\sigma\beta=0_{0,X}
, the latter being necessary from the universal property of the object
0
. Now, take any other map
\gamma\colon C\to X
, satisfying the same equations. Thus,
\pi\gamma=\beta=\pi\sigma\beta
. However, since
\pi
is split epi, this implies that
\gamma=\sigma\beta
. This proves that the diagram is a pushout. By applying this general argument to the fundamental short sequence of a tangent display map and using Theorems
2.31
and
3.21
, we conclude that a tangent display map equipped with an Ehresmann connection is necessarily a submersion.
∎

A splitting of the fundamental short exact sequence provides a decomposition of the tangent bundle
\mathbf{p}_{E}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to E
of
E
into a vertical component and a horizontal component, given by the Finsler bundle.

Corollary 3.23
.

An Ehresmann connection
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
on a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
makes the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{V}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{F}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{p}_{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{V}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{F}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{q}}

into a biproduct diagram in the category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
. In particular, it induces a linear isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbf{p}_{E}\cong\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\oplus_{E}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{F}}

which preserves the projections (or inclusions) of the biproduct, where
\oplus_{E}
denotes a biproduct in
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
. Conversely, a linear isomorphism between
\mathbf{p}_{E}\cong\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\oplus_{E}\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{F}}
which preserves the projections (or inclusions) of the biproduct induces an Ehresmann connection on
q
.

Proof.

This is a standard result of short exact sequences that extends readily to the context of the fundamental short exact sequence of submersions. We leave it to the reader to check the details of the proof.
∎

In a semi-additive category, giving a splitting of a short exact sequence requires specifying two pieces of information: a section
\sigma
and a retraction
\rho
. Thanks to the splitting lemma, this becomes redundant in an
additive category
, which is a semi-additive category enriched over the category of Abelian groups. The semi-additive category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
of differential bundles over
E
(
[
15
, Proposition 4.1]
2
2

2

Notice that the author uses
additive
to mean semi-additive.
) becomes additive when the base tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits negatives. This is a folklore result, but for completeness, we report it here.

Lemma 3.24
.

In a tangent category with negatives, the category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
of differential bundles over an object
E
and linear morphisms becomes an additive category.

Proof.

When the tangent structure admits negatives, using the universal property of the vertical lift of differential bundles, one can induce a negation morphism from the tangent bundle of the total space. Using this construction, one can see that the semi-additive category of differential bundles and linear morphisms over a fixed base object becomes additive.
∎

Thus, thanks to the exactness of the fundamental short exact sequence of a submersion, in the presence of negatives, giving an Ehresmann connection is equivalent to providing either a vertical or a horizontal connection.

Theorem 3.25
.

In a tangent category with negatives, for each vertical connection
\mathrm{R}
of a submersion
q
, there exists exactly one horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}
of
q
making
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}})
into an Ehresmann connection of
q
. Moreover, for each horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}
of
q
, there exists exactly one vertical connection
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
rendering the pair
(\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{H})
an Ehresmann connection on
q
.

Proof.

By the previous lemma, in the presence of negatives, the category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
is additive. We can then employ the splitting lemma. Explicitly, suppose that
\mathrm{R}
is a vertical connection. Since
q
is a submersion, we can use the universal property of the pushout diagram of Definition
2.29
and construct a unique morphism
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}
rendering the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}n_{E},\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}E}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}}

commutative in
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
, where we used negatives
n_{E}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}E
. From
\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}=\langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}n_{E},\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}E}\rangle s_{E}
. We immediately deduce that
\langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q},\mathrm{H}\pi_{q}\rangle s_{E}=p_{E}z_{E}
, that is,
[EC.2]
. Using the universal property of the pushout diagram for a submersion, and by the following computation

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}n_{E},\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}E}\rangle s_{E}\mathrm{R}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}\mathrm{R}n_{\mathrm{V}q},\mathrm{R}\rangle s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
\text{Linearity of }\mathrm{R}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\mathrm{R}n_{\mathrm{V}q},\mathrm{R}\rangle s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
[VC.1]
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ p_{E}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}

(
Unitality
)

we deduce that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{R}=q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}
, that is,
[EC.1]
.
[HC.2]
is also automatic, since
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}
is already a morphism of
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
, so, in particular, linear. Finally, to prove
[HC.1]
, we use the universal property of the pushout diagram again, together with the following computation:

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}n_{E},\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}E}\rangle s_{E}\pi_{q}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}\pi_{q}n_{\mathrm{F}q},\pi_{q}\rangle s_{\mathrm{F}q}

(
\text{Linearity of }\pi_{q}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle p_{E}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}n_{\mathrm{F}q},\pi_{q}\rangle s_{\mathrm{F}q}

(
\iota_{q}\pi_{q}=q^{\mathrm{V}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ p_{E}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}

(
Unitality
)

Thus,
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}})
is in fact an Ehresmann connection on
q
. Dually, if
\mathrm{H}
is a horizontal connection, we construct a vertical connection
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
as the unique morphism rendering the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}E},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}n_{E}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}}

Using dual arguments, one can easily show that
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
is in fact a vertical connection and that
(\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{H})
is in fact an Ehresmann connection. Finally, the uniqueness of
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}
and
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
comes from the universal property of the pullback and pushout diagram.
∎

From the stability under retraction of vertical and horizontal connections, it also follows that Ehresmann connections are also stable under retraction.

Theorem 3.26
.

In a tangent category whose tangent display maps are closed under retraction, Ehresmann connections are also closed under retraction.

Proof.

We leave it to the reader to complete this proof.
∎

3.5
Full connections

In
[
15
]
, Lucyshyn-Right presented an alternative approach to define an Koszul connection in tangent categories. In this section, we extend this point of view to Ehresmann connections. We introduce the notions of
full vertical
and
full horizontal connections
and prove an equivalence between full connections and Ehresmann connections. We begin by introducing the notion of a full vertical connection.

Definition 3.27
.

A
full vertical connection
on a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
consists of a vertical connection
\mathrm{R}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{V}q
on
q
subject to the following condition:

​[FVC]:

The following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{M}}

is a tangent pullback in the base tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

A full vertical connection suffices to define an Ehresmann connection.

Proposition 3.28
.

If
\mathrm{R}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{V}q
is a full vertical connection on a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
, there exists a unique horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
on
q
that completes
\mathrm{R}
into an Ehresmann connection
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}})
on
q
.

Proof.

Consider a full vertical connection
\mathrm{R}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{V}q
on
q
. Using
[FVC]
, we construct a (necessarily unique) morphism
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
which renders the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{M}}

commutative. To prove the existence of such a morphism
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}
, we first compute:

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}qz_{M}p_{M}=\iota_{q}\mathrm{T}qp_{M}=\iota_{q}p_{E}q

It follows that
q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}qp_{M}
:

\displaystyle q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\iota_{q}p_{E}q

(
\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q=\iota_{q}p_{E}q
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{E}p_{E}q

(
z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\iota_{q}=z_{E}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{F}}q

(
z_{E}p_{E}=\mathsf{id}_{E}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}qp_{M}

(
q^{\mathrm{F}}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}qp_{M}
)

Now, we shall prove that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
is, in fact, a horizontal connection on
q
. To begin, we show that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}p_{E}=q^{\mathrm{F}}
by computing the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}p_{E}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}p_{E}

(
p_{E}=\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}p_{E}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\iota_{q}p_{E}

(
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{R}=q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{E}p_{E}

(
z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\iota_{q}=z_{E}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{F}}

(
z_{E}p_{E}=\mathsf{id}_{E}
)

Next, we prove that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}
is linear, that is,
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}l_{E}=l_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}
. First, we compute the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}l_{E}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{R}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{R}l_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
l_{E}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{R}=\mathrm{R}l_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}l_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{R}=q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{E}\mathrm{T}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}l_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}=z\mathrm{T}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}
)

However, we can also compute:

\displaystyle l_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{R}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ l_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}q^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{R}=q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{E}\mathrm{T}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
l_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}q^{\mathrm{F}}=q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{E}
)

Furthermore:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}l_{E}\mathrm{T}^{2}q

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{T}ql_{M}

(
l_{E}\mathrm{T}^{2}q=\mathrm{T}ql_{M}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}ql_{M}

(
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{T}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q
)

and moreover:

\displaystyle l_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{T}^{2}q

\displaystyle=\penalty\ l_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q

(
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{T}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}ql_{M}

(
l_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}ql_{M}
)

Therefore, by invoking
[FVC]
, we conclude that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}l_{E}=l_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}
, that is, that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\colon\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\to\mathbf{p}_{E}
is a linear morphism. Finally, we prove that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{q}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{F}q}
. We compute:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{q}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{T}q

(
\pi_{q}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q=\mathrm{T}q
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q

(
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{T}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q
)

and also:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{q}q^{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}p_{E}

(
\pi_{q}q^{\mathrm{F}}=p_{E}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ q^{\mathrm{F}}

(
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}p_{E}=q^{\mathrm{F}}
)

Therefore, by the universal property of the Finsler bundle
\mathrm{F}q
,
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\pi_{q}
must coincide with the identity on
\mathrm{F}q
. So far, we have shown that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
is a horizontal connection on
q
. Furthermore,
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}
satisfies
[EC.1]
by construction. It is left to show that
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}})
verifies also
[EC.2]
. Let us compute the following:

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\rangle s_{E}\mathrm{R}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}\mathrm{R},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{R}\rangle s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
\mathrm{R}\text{ linear thus }s_{E}\mathrm{R}=(\mathrm{R}\times_{E}\mathrm{R})s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\mathrm{R},\pi_{q}q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\rangle s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
\iota_{q}\mathrm{R}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{V}q},\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{R}=q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{R}\langle\mathsf{id},\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}qz_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\rangle s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
\pi_{q}q^{\mathrm{F}}=p_{E}=\mathrm{R}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{R}

(
unitality
)

Moreover:

\displaystyle\langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\rangle s_{E}\mathrm{T}q

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}\mathrm{T}q,\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{T}q\rangle s_{M}

(
s_{E}\mathrm{T}q=\mathrm{T}_{2}qs_{M}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\mathrm{R}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}qz_{M},\pi_{q}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q\rangle s_{M}

(
\iota_{q}\mathrm{T}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}qz_{M},\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\mathrm{T}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}q\langle p_{M}z_{M},\mathsf{id}\rangle s_{M}

(
\mathrm{R}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q=\mathrm{T}qp,\pi_{q}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q=\mathrm{T}q
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{T}q

(
unitality
)

Therefore, by
[FVC]
,
\langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}\rangle s_{E}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}E}
, thus
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}})
is an Ehresmann connection. Uniqueness of
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}
follows from the universal property of the pullback diagram
[FVC]
.
∎

A full vertical connection is a vertical connection which satisfies
[FVC]
. Proposition
3.28
shows that every vertical connection subject to this extra condition can be completed uniquely into an Ehresmann connection. Similarly, starting from a horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}
, one would like to construct a vertical connection
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
that completes
\mathrm{H}
. The next definition introduces the extra assumption on a horizontal connection required to construct
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
.

Definition 3.29
.

A
full horizontal connection
on a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
is a horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
on
q
subject to the following condition:

​[FHC]:

The following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{0}_{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{V}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{q^{\mathrm{F}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{p}_{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

is a pushout diagram in the category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
.

As we proved for full vertical connections, a full horizontal connection is sufficient to define an Ehresmann connection.

Proposition 3.30
.

If
\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{V}q
is a full horizontal connection on a submersion
q\colon E\to M
, there exists a unique vertical connection
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{V}q
on
q
that completes
\mathrm{H}
into an Ehresmann connection
(\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{H})
on
q
.

Proof.

Consider a full horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}E
on
q
. We construct a (necessarily unique) morphism
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{V}q
which renders the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{0}_{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{V}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{F}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{p}_{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{0}_{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{q^{\mathrm{V}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\mathrm{V}}_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\mathrm{F}}_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\mathrm{V}}_{q}}

commutative. To prove the existence of such a morphism, notice that, since
\mathbf{0}_{E}
is a zero object in
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
, that is, it is both initial and terminal, there must exist a unique morphism
\mathbf{0}_{E}\to\mathbf{0}_{E}
. Thus,
z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}q^{\mathrm{F}}
coincides with the identity on
\mathbf{0}_{E}
. Now, we shall prove that
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
is in fact a vertical connection on
q
. Since the pushout diagram is in the category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};E)
of differential bundles over
E
and linear morphisms,
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}\colon\mathbf{p}_{E}\to\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{V}}
is automatically a linear morphism, thus,
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
satisfies
[VC.2]
. Furthermore, by construction,
\iota_{q}\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{V}q}
, thus,
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
verifies
[VC.1]
as well. Therefore,
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
is a vertical connection on
q
. Finally, we prove that
(\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{H})
is an Ehresmann connection. To this end, it is only left to prove
[EC.2]
, since
[EC.1]
holds by construction. Let us compute the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}\langle\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}\iota_{q},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}\rangle s

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\iota_{q},\mathrm{H}\rangle s_{E}

(
\mathrm{H}\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}=q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}},\mathrm{H}\pi_{q}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{F}q}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}\langle p_{E}z_{E},\mathsf{id}\rangle s_{E}

(
q^{\mathrm{F}}=\mathrm{H}p_{E},z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\iota_{q}=z_{E}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}

(
unitality
)

Moreover:

\displaystyle\iota_{q}\langle\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}\iota_{q},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}\rangle s_{E}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\iota_{q},\iota_{q}p_{E}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{H}\rangle s_{E}

(
\iota_{q}\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}=\mathsf{id},\iota_{q}\pi_{q}=\iota_{q}p_{E}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \iota_{q}\langle\mathsf{id},p_{E}z_{E}\rangle s_{E}

(
\mathrm{H}\text{ linear thus }z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\mathrm{H}=z
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \iota_{q}

(
unitality
)

By invoking
[FHC]
, we conclude that
\langle\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}\iota_{q},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}\rangle s_{E}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}E}
, that is,
(\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{H})
satisfies
[EC.2]
and it forms a Ehresmann connection. Uniqueness of
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
follows from the universal property of the pushout diagram
[FHC]
.
∎

Remark 3.31
.

In Proposition
3.28
, the map
q
is assumed to be only tangent display. This is not sufficient for Proposition
3.30
, in which
q
is in fact assumed to be a submersion, since we made use of the pushout of Equation (
2.3
) to construct
\mathrm{R}_{\mathrm{H}}
.

Propositions
3.28
and
3.30
show that a full vertical connection or a full horizontal connection suffices to construct an Ehresmann connection. The next result shows that the converse is also true, that is, the vertical and the horizontal components of an Ehresmann connection are always full.

Proposition 3.32
.

If
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
is an Ehresmann connection on a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
, then
\mathrm{R}
is a full vertical connection and
\mathrm{H}
is a full horizontal connection.

Proof.

Consider an Ehresmann connection
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
. We will prove that
\mathrm{R}
is full by induction. First, we show that
\mathrm{R}
verifies
[FVC]
. Consider two morphisms
f\colon X\to\mathrm{T}M
and
g\colon X\to\mathrm{V}q
making the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{M}}

commutative. We may denote by
\tilde{f}\colon X\to\mathrm{F}q
the unique morphism rendering the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{F}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\mathrm{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{M}}

commutative. Now, we define another morphism as follows:

\displaystyle h\colon X\xrightarrow{\langle g\iota_{q},\tilde{f}\mathrm{H}\rangle}\mathrm{T}_{2}E\xrightarrow{s_{E}}\mathrm{T}E

We want to prove that
h
is the unique morphism satisfying the following equations:

h\mathrm{R}=g\qquad h\mathrm{T}q=f

Let us start by showing that
h
is unique. Consider a morphism
h^{\prime}
satisfying the same equations, namely,
h^{\prime}\mathrm{R}=g
and
h^{\prime}\mathrm{T}q=f
. Thus:

\displaystyle h^{\prime}\pi_{q}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q

\displaystyle=\penalty\ h^{\prime}\mathrm{T}q

(
\pi_{q}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q=\mathrm{T}q
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ f

(
h^{\prime}\mathrm{T}q=f
)

Moreover:

\displaystyle h^{\prime}\pi_{q}q^{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ h^{\prime}\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}p_{E}

(
\pi_{q}q^{\mathrm{F}}=p_{E}=\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}p_{E}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ g\iota_{q}p_{E}

(
h^{\prime}\mathrm{R}=g
)

Thus, by the universal property of the Finsler bundle,
h^{\prime}\pi_{q}=\tilde{f}
. Thus:

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle g\iota_{q},\tilde{f}\mathrm{H}\rangle s_{E}

(
h=\langle g\iota_{q},\tilde{f}\mathrm{H}\rangle s_{E}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ h^{\prime}\langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}\rangle s_{E}

(
g=h^{\prime}\mathrm{R},\tilde{f}=h^{\prime}\pi_{q}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ h^{\prime}

(
[EC.2]
)

This proves the uniqueness of
h
. Now, we prove that
h
satisfies the desired equations:

\displaystyle h\mathrm{T}q

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle g\iota_{q},\tilde{f}\mathrm{H}\rangle s_{E}\mathrm{T}q

(
h=\langle g\iota_{q},\tilde{f}\mathrm{H}\rangle s_{E}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle g\iota_{q}\mathrm{T}q,\tilde{f}\mathrm{H}\mathrm{T}q\rangle s_{M}

(
s_{E}\mathrm{T}q=\mathrm{T}_{2}qs_{M}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle g\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}qz_{M},\tilde{f}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q\rangle s_{M}

(
\iota_{q}\mathrm{T}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}qz_{M},\mathrm{H}\mathrm{T}q=\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ f\langle p_{M}z_{M},\mathsf{id}\rangle s_{M}

(
g\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{V}}q=fp_{M},\tilde{f}\mathrm{T}^{\mathrm{F}}q=f
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ f

(
unitality
)

Furthermore:

\displaystyle h\mathrm{R}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle g\iota_{q},\tilde{f}\mathrm{H}\rangle s_{E}\mathrm{R}

(
h=\langle g\iota_{q},\tilde{f}\mathrm{H}\rangle s_{E}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle g\iota_{q}\mathrm{R},\tilde{f}\mathrm{H}\mathrm{R}\rangle s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
\mathrm{R}\text{ linear thus }s_{E}\mathrm{R}=(\mathrm{R}\times_{E}\mathrm{R})s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle g,\tilde{f}q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\rangle s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
\iota_{q}\mathrm{R}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{V}q},\mathrm{H}\mathrm{R}=q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ g\langle\mathsf{id},q^{\mathrm{V}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\rangle s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}

(
\tilde{f}q^{\mathrm{F}}=gq^{\mathrm{V}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ g

(
unitality
)

This proves that the diagram of
[FVC]
is a pullback. To prove that this is a tangent pullback, consider an integer
n>0
. If
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
is an Ehresmann connection on
q
,
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{EC}}(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
is an Ehresmann connection on
\mathrm{T}q
, where
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{EC}}
denotes the tangent bundle functor of the tangent category
\mathsf{EC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of Ehresmann connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Thus, by induction,
{\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{EC}}}^{n}(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
is an Ehresmann connection on
\mathrm{T}^{n}q
. The vertical connection part of this Ehresmann connection can be written as
\gamma_{n}\mathrm{T}^{n}\mathrm{R}
, where
\gamma_{n}
is the isomorphism:

\displaystyle\gamma_{n}=c_{\mathrm{T}^{n}}\mathrm{T}c_{\mathrm{T}^{n-1}}\cdot{\dots}\cdot\mathrm{T}^{k}c_{\mathrm{T}^{n-k}}\cdot{\dots}\cdot\mathrm{T}^{n}c

We have already proven that the vertical connection
\mathrm{R}
of an Ehresmann connection
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
renders the diagram of
[FVC]
into a pullback. Applying the same argument for
{\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{EC}}}^{n}(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
, we conclude that the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{n+1}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}\mathrm{T}^{n}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{n+1}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{n}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{n}\mathrm{T}^{n}\mathrm{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{n+1}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathrm{T}^{n}q}p_{\mathrm{T}^{n}E}\mathrm{T}^{n}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\mathrm{T}^{n}M}}

is also a pullback. However, by definition of
\gamma_{n}
and by the properties of the canonical flip,
p_{\mathrm{T}^{n}}=\gamma_{n}\mathrm{T}^{n}p
. Furthermore, using the invertibility of
\gamma_{n}
and
\gamma_{n-1}
, the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{n+1}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{n+1}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}\mathrm{T}^{n}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{n}\mathrm{V}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{n+1}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{n+1}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{n}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{n}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{n+1}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{n}\mathrm{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{n+1}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathrm{T}^{n}q}p_{\mathrm{T}^{n}E}\mathrm{T}^{n}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{n}(\iota_{q}p_{E}q)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{n}p_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{n-1}}

is also a pullback. Therefore, the central square diagram must also be a pullback, that is,
\mathrm{R}
satisfies
[FVC]
. The final step is to prove that
\mathrm{H}
is a full horizontal connection, that is,
\mathrm{H}
satisfies
[FHC]
. Consider a differential bundle
\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\colon X\to E
over
E
and two linear morphisms of differential bundles
f\colon\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{F}}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
and
g\colon\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
, making the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{0}_{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{q^{\mathrm{V}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{q^{\mathrm{F}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{p}_{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{q}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

commutative. We may define the following morphism:

\displaystyle h\colon

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{\langle\mathrm{R}g,\pi_{q}f\rangle}X_{2}\xrightarrow{s_{q^{\prime}}}X

We leave it to the reader to prove that
h
is a linear morphism of differential bundles. We shall prove that
h
is the unique morphism satisfying
\mathrm{H}h=f
and
\iota_{q}h=g
. We begin by proving uniqueness. Consider a morphism
h^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to X
which satisfies the same equations, that is,
\mathrm{H}h^{\prime}=f
and
\iota_{q}h^{\prime}=g
. We compute:

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\mathrm{R}g,\pi_{q}f\rangle s_{q^{\prime}}

(
h=\langle\mathrm{R}g,\pi_{q}f\rangle s_{q^{\prime}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q}h^{\prime},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}h^{\prime}\rangle s_{q^{\prime}}

(
g=\iota_{q}h^{\prime},f=\mathrm{H}h^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\mathrm{R}\iota_{q},\pi_{q}\mathrm{H}\rangle sh^{\prime}

(
h^{\prime}\text{ linear thus }(h^{\prime}\times_{E}h^{\prime})s_{q^{\prime}}=sh^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ h^{\prime}

(
[EC.2]
)

Let us now prove that
h
satisfies the desired equations:

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}h

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{H}\langle\mathrm{R}g,\pi_{q}f\rangle s_{q^{\prime}}

(
h=\langle\mathrm{R}g,\pi_{q}f\rangle s_{q^{\prime}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}g,f\rangle s_{q^{\prime}}

(
\mathrm{H}\mathrm{R}=q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}},\mathrm{H}\pi_{q}=\mathsf{id}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}},\mathsf{id}\rangle(f\times_{E}f)s_{q^{\prime}}

(
z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}g=z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}f
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle q^{\mathrm{F}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}},\mathsf{id}\rangle s_{\mathrm{F}q}f

(
f\text{ linear thus }(f\times_{E}f)s_{q^{\prime}}=s_{\mathrm{F}q}f
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ f

(
unitality
)

Moreover:

\displaystyle\iota_{q}h

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \iota_{q}\langle\mathrm{R}g,\pi_{q}f\rangle s_{q^{\prime}}

(
h=\langle\mathrm{R}g,\pi_{q}f\rangle s_{q^{\prime}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle g,q^{\mathrm{V}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}f\rangle s_{q^{\prime}}

(
\iota_{q}\mathrm{R}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{V}q},\iota_{q}\pi_{q}=q^{\mathrm{V}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle g,q^{\mathrm{V}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}g\rangle s_{q^{\prime}}

(
z_{q}^{\mathrm{F}}f=z\pi_{q}f=z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}g
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \langle\mathsf{id},q^{\mathrm{V}}z_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\rangle s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}g

(
g\text{ linear thus }(g\times_{E}g)s_{q^{\prime}}=s_{q}^{\mathrm{V}}g
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ g

(
unitality
)

This concludes the proof.
∎

We previosuly showed that full vertical and horizontal connections can always be completed uniquely into an Ehresmann connection. We also proved that the vertical and horizontal components of a Ehresmann connections are always full. This allows us to put all the pieces together into the following result:

Theorem 3.33
.

Given a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
, the following are equivalent:

[1]

\mathrm{R}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{V}q
is a full vertical connection on
q
and
q
is a submersion;

[2]

\mathrm{R}
is the vertical component of an Ehresmann connection
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
on
q
.

Furthermore, the following are also equivalent:

[1]

\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{F}q\to\mathrm{T}W
is a full horizontal connection on
q
and
q
is a submersion;

[2]

\mathrm{H}
is the horizontal component of an Ehresmann connection
(\mathrm{R},\mathrm{H})
on
q
.

3.6
Abstract connections

In the previous section, we showed that full vertical connections, full horizontal connections, and Ehresmann connections are in fact equivalent concepts. In this section, we introduce another new perspective: abstract connections.

Our approach so far was to introduce the concepts of vertical and Finsler bundle of a tangent display map, and later used them to define vertical and horizontal connections. Notice that both the vertical and the Finsler bundle are only specified up to a unique isomorphism, since they are constructed using the universal properties of tangent pullbacks. Therefore, choosing a vertical connection or a horizontal connection on a tangent display map
q
implicitly requires making a choice of either the vertical or the Finsler bundle of
q
.

We then showed that a vertical connection defines an idempotent, the
vertical connection form
, and that a horizontal connection is naturally associated with another idempotent, the
horizontal connection form
. The vertical connection form
\phi_{\mathrm{R}}
of a vertical connection
\mathrm{R}
splits on the vertical bundle, and the horizontal connection form
\phi_{\mathrm{H}}
of a horizontal connection
\mathrm{H}
splits on the Finsler bundle.

In this section, we introduce the notions of
abstract
vertical connections and
abstract
horizontal connections. An abstract vertical connection on a
map

q\colon E\to M
consists of a linear idempotent on the tangent bundle of
E
, satisfying an extra universal property. Similarly, an abstract horizontal connection on
q
consists of another linear idempotent of
\mathbf{p}_{E}
, satisfying another universal property. Crucially, no mention of the vertical or the Finsler bundle is made in defining these concepts, nor is the map
q
required to be tangent display. A
splitting
of an abstract vertical connection gives both a choice of the vertical bundle of
q
and a vertical connection on it. A
splitting
of an abstract horizontal connection gives both a choice of the Finsler bundle of
q
and a horizontal connection on it.

The universal properties satisfied by an abstract vertical connection and an abstract horizontal connection directly link to the axioms
[FVC]
and
[FHC]
of full connections. To begin, we define abstract vertical connections.

Definition 3.34
.

An
abstract vertical connection
on a map
q\colon E\to M
consists of a morphism
\phi\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}E
subject to the following conditions:

​[AVC.1]:

\phi
is an idempotent, that is,
\phi\phi=\phi
;

​[AVC.2]:

\phi\colon\mathbf{p}_{E}\to\mathbf{p}_{E}
is a linear morphism of differential bundles, that is,
\phi p_{E}=p_{E}
and
\phi l_{E}=l_{E}\mathrm{T}\phi
;

​[AVC.3]:

The following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{M}}

commutes and is a tangent pullback diagram in the base tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

An idempotent
e\colon A\to A
in a category
\mathbb{X}

splits
when there exists an object
B
of
\mathbb{X}
together with a section-retraction pair
(s,r)\colon B\to A
, that is,
s\colon B\to A
and
r\colon A\to B
satisfying
sr=\mathsf{id}_{B}
, such that
e=rs
. If
(B,s,r)
and
(B^{\prime},s^{\prime},r^{\prime})
are two splittings of an idempotent
e
, there exists a unique isomorphism
\varphi\colon B\to B^{\prime}
satisfying
r\varphi=r^{\prime}
.

We aim to show a correspondence between
linear
splittings of an abstract vertical connection and a choice of the vertical bundle together with a full vertical connection on it. First, let us define the notion of linear splittings.

Definition 3.35
.

A
linear idempotent
in a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
on an object
M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
consists of a morphism
\phi\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}E
, compatible with the tangent bundles, that is,
\phi p_{E}=p_{E}
, which is an idempotent in the category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};M)
of differential bundles over
M
. A
linear splitting
of a linear idempotent
\phi
consists of a splitting of
\phi
in
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};M)
. A linear idempotent
splits
provided there exists at least one linear splitting of it.

Definition 3.36
.

An abstract vertical connection
\phi\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}E
on a map
q\colon E\to M
splits
provided the linear idempotent
\phi
of
E
splits.

Lemma 3.37
.

Let
\mathbb{X}
be a category equipped with an endofunctor
\mathrm{T}\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
. Consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

of
\mathbb{X}
, where
(s,r)\colon A\to B
and
(s^{\prime},r^{\prime})\colon A\to B^{\prime}
are two section-retraction pairs. Then, the outer square is a
\mathrm{T}
-pullback if and only if the left and the right squares are both
\mathrm{T}
-pullbacks.

Proof.

This is a standard categorical argument, and left to the reader.
∎

Lemma 3.38
.

If
\phi\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}E
is a split abstract vertical connection on a map
q\colon E\to M
and
(\mathbf{q}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{V}},\iota_{\phi},\mathrm{R}_{\phi})
is a splitting of
\phi
, then the differential bundle
\mathbf{q}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{V}}\colon\mathrm{V}_{\phi}q\to E
is a choice of the vertical bundle of
q
.

Proof.

Consider a split abstract vertical connection
\phi
on
q
and a linear splitting
(\mathbf{q}_{\phi}^{\mathrm{V}},\iota_{\phi},\mathrm{R}_{\phi})
of
\phi
. Moreover, consider the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{V}_{\phi}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\phi}p_{E}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{M}}

(3.2)

First, we prove that this diagram commutes. By
[AVC.3]
, the outer square commutes. Moreover, we compute:

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}qp_{M}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ p_{E}q

(
\mathrm{T}qp_{M}=p_{E}q
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \phi p_{E}q

(
p_{E}=\phi p_{E}
)

\displaystyle=\penalty\ \mathrm{R}_{\phi}\iota_{\phi}p_{E}q

(
\phi=\mathrm{R}_{\phi}\iota_{\phi}
)

To prove that the right square commutes, we compute:

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}_{\phi}\iota_{\phi}\mathrm{T}q=\mathrm{T}qp_{M}z_{M}=\mathrm{R}_{\phi}\iota_{\phi}p_{E}qz_{M}

Since
\iota_{\phi}\mathrm{R}_{\phi}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{V}_{\phi}q}
thus, by applying
\iota_{\phi}
on both sides we obtain that,
\iota_{\phi}\mathrm{T}q=\iota_{\phi}p_{E}qz_{M}
, that is, the right square commutes.

Since by
[AVC.3]
, the outer square is a tangent pullback, by Lemma
3.37
, so are the two inner square diagrams. In particular, the left square diagram is a tangent pullback. Therefore, by using the same construction as in Section
2.2
, we can equip the map
\iota_{\phi}p_{E}\colon\mathrm{V}_{\phi}q\to E
with the structure of a differential bundle. Our goal is to show that this differential bundle, that represents a choice of the vertical bundle for
q
, coincides with the differential bundle
\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{V}}_{\phi}\colon\mathrm{V}_{\phi}q\to E
, that comes directly from the linear splitting of
\phi
. Notice that
\iota_{\phi}\colon\mathbf{q}^{\mathrm{V}}\to\mathbf{p}_{E}
is a linear tangent monic (that is
